My question is: 59/M “What steps will you take to give more attention to the topic of multi-way and thus give consumers freedom of choice between multi-way and disposable bottles?” This is why I am moving this question – and not only today, on the International Day of the Earth, but in general; and not only me, but also many spectators – because we are very concerned that Austria is relatively far away from the climate commitments we have entered, and we are only making a very, very small contribution to limiting climate change, climate change. At the same time, we know that 415 000 tonnes of CO2 equivalent come only from the area of beverage packaging, of which 80 percent of disposable bottles. The increase in the multi-way ratio would be an effective contribution to climate protection and would give many people in Austria the freedom of choice between disposable and multi-way bottles. There are currently no multi-way bottles on the shelves.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-04-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00062_d7e2733	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-04-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00062_d7e2748	Ladies and gentlemen! High house! I can also wish you a good morning. On the topic of multiway: Yes, I am also in favour of freedom of choice for consumers, and the citizens have also opted for it. In surveys, a vast majority of citizens say yes, they want to buy multi-way bottles. – In practice, however, it is completely reversed. In practice, a vast majority of citizens decide against the multi-way system. I can also explain this to you by means of numbers. 13 years ago, 90 percent were bought by multi-way in the field of water multi-way packaging, and today it is only 19 percent. For the lemonades it was 43 percent, today it is only 9 percent. Overall, a drop of 50 per cent to 20 per cent is recorded in the majority; that is, consumers prefer to make one move. I am not concerned that multi-way is a self-interest, but it must vote on the overall environmental balance. We have reached a voluntary agreement with the economy, but the economy has committed itself to optimising the overall ecosystem and reducing greenhouse gases and emissions. We are currently in the evaluation phase, and waste management is one of the few sectors where we are today achieving the Kyoto targets for climate protection, because there are 40 percent greenhouse gases.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-04-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00062_d7e2891	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2010-04-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00062_d7e2906	Ladies and gentlemen, we have around 1.1 million tonnes of annual packaging. It is crucial that there is a proper resource management, so, as I said earlier, as much as possible comes back to the fabric cycle, and this also succeeds: About 800 000 tonnes of 1.1 million are returned to the dust cycle: It is in the very sense of sustainable management of our waste. So it is not stored somewhere, it is not simply burned, as other countries in the world do, but traced back. I may be able to document this on the basis of the use of glass by mentioning a few impressive numbers. By collecting glass and returning to the fabric cycle, we save 158 000 tons of quartz sand, 51 000 tons of lime, 217 million kilowatt hours of electrical energy, 5.7 million cubic meters of natural gas. The amount of energy we save is enough for 33,000 households. Therefore, from this point of view, a warm thanks to the citizens for their involvement and proper return of substances to the fabric cycle. As has been said, we unfortunately do not achieve the Kyoto targets in many sectors. We reach them today in agriculture, and we reach them in the waste management sector, because there is 40 percent of greenhouse gases that have been reduced since 1990.

Especially you always demand that we support small businesses. The ÖVP also does so, because the tax fixture is used especially for small farmers. As you know, the large farms are subject to accounting. In between, the holdings that have a profit assessment from € 65 000 to € 150 000 are part of a revenue-expenditure account. But the larger farms have to make a bookkeeping. That means, especially for small businesses, this system is important. I and also the Minister of Finance, who is responsible for the assessment law, are concerned that we keep this system because it saves on the one hand small farmers costs and on the other hand saves the financial management control costs because it is simplified. We want to keep this system safe because we want to give the farmers security.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-04-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00062_d7e6818	Madam President. Mr. Vice-Chancellor! Dear Members of the Government! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! A preview has a pre- and one disadvantage: It has the advantage of preparing such a thing as a medium-term policy under certain assumptions, but it also has the disadvantage of starting from assumptions up to 2014, which cannot be said at the hour if they will happen. No one would have been able five years ago to predict this year properly, to make the assumptions in such a way that they conform to today's circumstances. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: This is unique what you are doing! A reduction in the debt forecast by the economic researchers can only be achieved if something is done on both the revenue and the expenditure side. The fact that the population will ask whether it is in line with both revenue and expenditure measures is something that we are responsible for. [[Abg. May. Kogler: They ask the same way!]] The Court of First Instance means that those who have contributed less in the past but who have been more able to do so, in particular in the case of revenue-based measures, under the examination of social justice, are being used more strongly. Strengthening education, strengthening the economy in relation to research, development, securing social networks that guarantee prosperity, ensuring health systems are not cheaper in the crisis – only the revenues are lower. We want to lay down in a number of areas, which is why we are required to become more efficient in each sector. Efficiency is a way to distribute something elsewhere. Expenditure between areas created by double-tracking, bureaucracy, perhaps by services that are no longer so necessary, should be called into question in order to have capacity for the actual tasks of material, financial, content. We also have the second lowest unemployment and youth unemployment because there is purchasing power because the population is able to keep the economy in many areas in motion even at this difficult time for the domestic market. This is no longer the case in other countries. That is, the strengths of our small businesses, our SMEs, our investments, the purchasing power in our country are something that helps us to have the second lowest unemployment in Europe. It is therefore also necessary to measure all these measures in order to ensure that they are effective in terms of the essential social security and employment policies. Because one thing is sure: No policy – nor revenue – can be better, stronger and more long-term than growth. Growth is the only chance to bring a country back out of the crisis and to increase employment in the medium and long term. We have deleted them – something you have introduced. Tax privileges for managers have also been removed. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] These measures were successful – they were very successful. It must be said that this federal government has acted very successfully here in the crisis when one looks at the way Austria stands compared to other countries in the European Union. Let us look at what unemployment looks like: Austria has the second lowest unemployment rate within the European Union. In the case of youth unemployment, we have the third lowest unemployment rate throughout the European Union. And the tax reform has, above all, prevented one from: After exports have broken down after private investment has broken down, we have made it through public investment and, above all, through the private consumption through the tax reform, that domestic consumption remains stable and that there are no more jobs lost here and an even greater economic downturn is taking place, as in many other countries. – Today, economic researchers are predicting growth for this year and for the next few years. Now is the time to clarify three questions. The first, in fact, is to draw the lessons from the crisis: How do we prevent the next time? The second question is how we can further strengthen the economy and growth. And the third question is of course: How do we finance the costs of this crisis? How do we finance the expenditure we had to spend in order to minimise the impact of the crisis? But if you look at the figures, you can see that Austria is in absolute top position in the European average. We are at the budget deficit seventh best, and at the end of 2009 we have a debt ratio of 66.5 percent – that is the seventeenth lowest. I think that we have also clearly defined our objective of reducing the deficit quickly and returning to the criteria of the EU Stability Pact. Openly, only the way to this goal is the way, and in this way we will also make massive efforts by the economy to ensure that the debate on expenditure-side savings potential enters the same dynamics as in the inventions of new taxes – I think that is also very important. We need clear signals for savings and structural reforms in bureaucracy, in the school and health systems. Unfortunately, we will only achieve efficiency improvements with open and transparent discussions. It is also important to start right now. Sepp Pröll said it yesterday and today: First save and increase efficiency, then secure growth and generate new growth. The economy needs this growth to secure jobs and the location. Ideas for more growth and impulses, as well as proposals for efficiency gains, are at the table. Starting instead of waiting is our foreign exchange.

ParlaMint-AT_2010-04-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00062_d7e11940	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! I will try to explain these polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons and what is behind them. Plastics accompany us in everyday life, we find them everywhere: in the car, in the children's toys, in plastic kitchenware as well as in handles of tools or in sports equipment. They are known to us in everyday use as rather elastic, formal mass. In reality, however, the origin of the plastics is hard and brittle, and in order to make them elastic, softeners must be added during the production or processing of plastics or rubber parts. There are, of course, different chemicals: some that are classified as safe and others, such as cheap polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons, which are classified as harmful to health. And these plasticizers are also the subject of this motion for a resolution today. PACs come through skin contact or through food intake into the human organism. They are fat-resolving, also collect in the human fat tissue and then cause serious health disorders. PACs are suspected of changing the inheritance, being carcinogenic and also affecting reproduction. And PAKs can penetrate into the body above all through skin contact. It takes 30 seconds to get in touch with these plasticizers, which are included in rubber parts or plastic articles. If you sweat or even use hand creams or cosmetics, the absorption of these PAKs even increases. There is a test of the Stiftung Warentest. This one has such cheap tools – and “aromatically” they call themselves because they also smell extremely; one can even smell these PACs in new plastic items – which are fitted with rubber parts, examine and detect at the handle: If you work with such a tool for an hour with an unprotected hand and are in contact with this rubber part, PAK enters the body in a lot as if this man or this woman had consumed 3 500 cigarettes. So you can see that these values are huge. And if you know that there are no specific standardisations across the EU, there are no limit values to the appropriate extent, then of course this motion for a resolution has its right, and I ask you to support it as well.

This scheme has two objectives: first, customers affected by the inability to pay the organisers during the journey to ensure a return trip as easily as possible; and Secondly, to replace customer funds paid before a journey that was no longer carried out. The transposition of this provision into national law takes place in the travel agency security regulation. In the specific case, increased payments were requested and paid by the customers who were not fully reimbursed after the bankruptcy. From the point of view of consumers, the evaluation of the travel agency security regulation to be decided today must ensure that compliance with the package travel directive is also ensured in order to prevent damage to consumers even in the event of inability to pay and in the event of bankruptcy. Unfortunately, users have no chance to see in advance what additives are inside and to protect themselves from any allergies – even if they are known – because they do not know whether these certain parts, to which they are allergic, are contained in the product or not. That is exactly what this request is about to do. This declaration should be implemented at European level if it is possible. If this is not possible, the Environment Ministers' proposal also calls on Austria to be active and, on the one hand, to keep an eye on and evaluate the studies that exist, for example, on the follow-up of allergies due to fragrances. If he believes that there are not enough studies and data anywhere, then we also call on him to do something himself, to commission studies, to make himself smart and, depending on what comes out, to become active – either at European or at national level. I think that's an important step. We know that more and more people suffer very much from such allergies due to fragrances. The amazing thing is that there are now more than 3,000 individual substances that are sometimes mixed together. It is worth knowing: There are only 15 substances worldwide that are truly relevant in quantity and are also tested and tested. The remaining nearly 3,000 substances are not researched. They are, above all, not really controlled because they are mixed. About 30 to 50 individual substances are used to produce a single fragrance. Unfortunately, what we need to notice more and more often is that allergic reactions occur. Similar to nickel allergies in the field of jewellery, these fragrances in the room air become increasingly contact allergies. The sad issue is that we can't really escape these fragrances anymore. Whether in department stores, in office premises or partly in waiting rooms of doctors or other facilities – you are often asked whether you want or not. There are groups to be protected, regardless of whether pregnant women or infants, who cannot escape this effect and in which allergic reactions are increasingly experienced. Current investigations show us. That is why I think this is a sensible resolution, which is being tabled today, which really is about evaluating the studies and findings that exist and perhaps commissioning new ones to find a uniform procedure in the declaration across Europe. Thank you.

Why do we need that? – The SAR value is in itself only part of the problem. The main problem in cell phone calls is not heat development, although it can be relatively unpleasant on the ear, but the main problem is the non-electronic effects, the biological effects, the non-heat effects, the effects caused by the transmission power in our nerve cells. And the SAR value doesn't say anything about that. However, we know that if there is a high load, then probably the so-called non-thermal effects, the biological effects are also relatively high. And that we should take this seriously, says, for example, your colleague Stöger, Mr. Federal Minister, I have already said it – a study from Denmark, which clearly explains that mothers who were often phoned with the mobile phone during pregnancy and who were exposed to different SAR values – that was a long-term study that went on for over ten years – have children who, because they were exposed to cell phone calls as embryos, are nervously weaker and have behavioural disorders rather than children who were not exposed to this burden during their embryo. This is a valid study, and it should be taken seriously. And that's why it's worth me to keep discussing this with you again and again. The ministry, the public hand has the coordinate system. Each transmission mast is to be located through the geographical coordinate system, through the terrestrial system. Only: Information for the layman says the coordinates – I don't know, 35. Latitude, 44. Longitude and then the minutes and seconds – nothing. Only the fight for information, in which place, in which street which transmission mast stands with what transmission power, is an infinite one. You like that? I don't like that! I will continue to work in this direction, and this information must also be ensured in the case of an amendment to the Telecommunications Act. This is the one scene, which I think is essential to information policy, because it is also about the studies that can better assess the effects of epidemiological nature. The other site is the eternal Scientific Advisory Council Funk. That is why I mention this because we talked about the budget this morning. This is how the work in this complex field looks mobile. I have now given you three examples: a very clear and two serious ones, where we must continue to work politically. I am also referring to the Citizens' Initiative No. 18 from the collection report. This citizens' initiative calls for the 2006 EU Directive, which requires all EU members to ensure that all telephone and Internet providers are suspected of storing all the connections and location data of their customers for six months. The citizens' initiative as a means of direct democracy shows how critical the people are about this issue. It is and remains an extremely sensitive issue – in Austria as well as in the EU. The four opinions received show a differentiated approach to this matter. Expert groups and the ministries also propose a minimum transposition or a significant fundamental rights-representative implementation, and the Data Protection Council has also requested an evaluation and re-evaluation of this Directive at EU level. All petitions and citizens' initiatives in the collective report show how important it is that various citizens' concerns in the form of petitions and citizens' initiatives really find their way into Parliament. In this way they will be discussed and dealt with as part of direct democracy at the highest political level, and in my opinion this is also the important task of this committee.

This sounds rather simple at first – my previous speakers have mentioned it in part – but of course you have to devote yourself to this matter deeper. It is important to me that in this context: Civil service is, according to the Constitution, a military service for those responsible for military service who for conscientious reasons refuse to use force of arms against people. The Polit But said it was a uniformly organized armed and militaryly organized civilian guard. And in the executive service, the need for the use of weapons against other persons, of course, within the meaning of the right to use weapons, can arise at any time. Consequently, the free choice of profession is relativized for the same reasons of conscience with which the military officer has made his decision for the military service because he has rejected the use of arms force against people. Therefore, for this very reason, one must go deeper into this matter and deal with these so-called reasons of conscience – one can now, as it were, make a change of conscience and withdraw this reasoning, thus re-entering the obligation to do so – more precisely. This is not so easy to say, it is about the Constitution, it is about fundamental things. On the other hand, the Ministry of the Interior is preparing proposals which include many other areas of civil service and which also deal with the subject of the declaration of conscience. But this is to be discussed in peace, with great materiality and with no emotion – in the sense of all those concerned, both the federal army, the police and the many young people who go on the path of civil service. Thank you.
